Processing these lightcurves involves filtering, detrending, and signal extraction.\n\n## Data Format\nTypical TESS lightcurve columns:\n- **Time**: Usually in MJD (Modified Julian Date) or BJD-TDB\n- **Flux**: Normalized or relative flux values\n- **Quality flags**: 0 = good data, non-zero = potentially problematic\n- **Flux uncertainty**: Error on each measurement\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install numpy pandas matplotlib scipy\n```\n\n## Loading and Filtering Data\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Load data\ndata = np.loadtxt('tess_lc.txt', skiprows=4)  # Skip header rows\ntime = data[:, 0]\nflux = data[:, 1]\nquality = data[:, 2]\nflux_err = data[:, 3]\n\n# Filter by quality flag (keep only good data)\ngood_mask = quality == 0\ntime = time[good_mask]\nflux = flux[good_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[good_mask]\n\n# Remove NaN values\nvalid_mask = ~np.isnan(flux) & ~np.isnan(time)\ntime = time[valid_mask]\nflux = flux[valid_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[valid_mask]\n```\n\n## Outlier Removal\n\n### Sigma Clipping\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef sigma_clip(data, sigma=3, maxiters=5):\n    \"\"\"Remove outliers using iterative sigma clipping.\"\"\"\n    mask = np.ones(len(data), dtype=bool)\n    for _ in range(maxiters):\n        mean = np.nanmean(data[mask])\n        std = np.nanstd(data[mask])\n        mask = np.abs(data - mean) < sigma * std\n    return mask\n\n# Apply sigma clipping to flux\nclip_mask = sigma_clip(flux, sigma=5)\ntime = time[clip_mask]\nflux = flux[clip_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[clip_mask]\n```\n\n### MAD-based Clipping (more robust)\n```python\ndef mad_clip(data, threshold=5):\n    \"\"\"Remove outliers using Median Absolute Deviation.\"\"\"\n    median = np.median(data)\n    mad = np.median(np.abs(data - median))\n    mad_sigma = 1.4826 * mad  # Convert MAD to sigma equivalent\n    mask = np.abs(data - median) < threshold * mad_sigma\n    return mask\n```\n\n## Normalization\n```python\n# Normalize flux to median\nmedian_flux = np.median(flux)\nflux_norm = flux / median_flux\nflux_err_norm = flux_err / median_flux\n```\n\n## Time Handling\n```python\n# Convert to relative time (from start of observations)\ntime_rel = time - time.min()\n\n# Calculate time baseline\nbaseline = time.max() - time.min()  # Total observation span in days\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Always filter by quality flags first\n2. Remove outliers before detrending\n3. Keep track of masks for reversibility\n4. Check for gaps in time series\n5. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01CJ6PNtaYH8kHmkikZZjLTN","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_012e4HQCWTfFe8cowWHMbwHU","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/stellar-variability-removal.md","content":"# Stellar Variability Removal for Exoplanet Detection\n\n## Overview\nStellar activity (starspots, rotation, pulsations) creates variability in lightcurves that can mask planetary transit signals. Detrending removes these long-term variations while preserving short-duration transit events.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install numpy scipy wotan\n# Optional: pip install celerite2 george\n```\n\n## Methods\n\n### 1. Savitzky-Golay Filter\nGood for smooth, slowly-varying trends.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.signal import savgol_filter\n\ndef detrend_savgol(flux, window_length=101, polyorder=3):\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply Savitzky-Golay filter for detrending.\n    window_length must be odd and > polyorder\n    \"\"\"\n    # Ensure window is odd\n    if window_length % 2 == 0:\n        window_length += 1\n\n    trend = savgol_filter(flux, window_length, polyorder)\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 2. Moving Median Filter\nSimple and robust to outliers.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.ndimage import median_filter\n\ndef detrend_median(flux, size=101):\n    \"\"\"Detrend using moving median.\"\"\"\n    trend = median_filter(flux, size=size)\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 3. Wotan Library (Recommended for Exoplanets)\nSpecialized library for transit photometry detrending.\n\n```python\nfrom wotan import flatten\n\n# Biweight filter (robust to transits)\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='biweight',\n                           window_length=0.5,  # days\n                           return_trend=True)\n\n# Alternative: Gaussian Process\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='gp',\n                           kernel='matern',\n                           kernel_size=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 4. Spline Fitting\nFlexible polynomial fitting.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.interpolate import UnivariateSpline\n\ndef detrend_spline(time, flux, s=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detrend using smoothing spline.\n    s: smoothing factor (None = automatic)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by time\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(time)\n    time_sorted = time[sort_idx]\n    flux_sorted = flux[sort_idx]\n\n    # Fit spline\n    spline = UnivariateSpline(time_sorted, flux_sorted, s=s)\n    trend = spline(time)\n\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 5. Iterative Detrending (Masking Transits)\nFor better results when transits are visible.\n\n```python\ndef iterative_detrend(time, flux, window=0.5, n_iter=3, sigma=3):\n    \"\"\"\n    Iteratively detrend while masking transit-like dips.\n    \"\"\"\n    mask = np.ones(len(flux), dtype=bool)\n\n    for _ in range(n_iter):\n        # Fit trend only to non-transit points\n        from wotan import flatten\n        detrended, trend = flatten(time[mask], flux[mask],\n                                   method='biweight',\n                                   window_length=window,\n                                   return_trend=True)\n\n        # Interpolate trend to all points\n        from scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n        trend_func = interp1d(time[mask], trend,\n                             fill_value='extrapolate')\n        full_trend = trend_func(time)\n\n        # Update mask (exclude deep dips)\n        residual = flux / full_trend\n        median_res = np.median(residual)\n        std_res = np.std(residual)\n        mask = residual > median_res - sigma * std_res\n\n    return flux / full_trend, full_trend\n```\n\n## Choosing Window Size\n- **Too small**: Will remove transit signals\n- **Too large**: Won't capture stellar variability\n- **Rule of thumb**: Window should be ~3-5x transit duration\n- For unknown transits: Start with 0.5-1.0 days\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use methods robust to outliers (biweight, median)\n2. Check that transits aren't being removed\n3. Compare multiple detrending methods\n4. Window size should match stellar variability timescale\n5. It's specifically designed for box-shaped (transit-like) signals that are brief compared to the orbital period.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install astropy scipy numpy\n# Optional: pip install transitleastsquares\n```\n\n## Using Astropy's BLS\n\n### Basic Usage\n```python\nfrom astropy.timeseries import BoxLeastSquares\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Create BLS model\nmodel = BoxLeastSquares(time, flux, dy=flux_err)\n\n# Define period grid\n# Rule: minimum period > 2x longest gap in data\n# Maximum period < half the baseline\nbaseline = time.max() - t0) % period) / period\n    phase[phase > 0.5] -= 1  # Center transit at phase 0\n    return phase\n\n# Fold on best period\nphase = phase_fold(time, best_period, best_t0)\n\n# Plot folded lightcurve\nplt.figure(figsize=(10, 4))\nplt.scatter(phase, flux, s=1, alpha=0.5)\nplt.xlabel('Phase')\nplt.ylabel('Normalized Flux')\nplt.title(f'Phase-folded at P = {best_period:.5f} days')\nplt.savefig('phase_folded.png', dpi=150)\n```\n\n## Using Transit Least Squares (TLS)\nMore sensitive than BLS, uses realistic transit shape.\n\n```python\nfrom transitleastsquares import transitleastsquares\n\n# Run TLS\nmodel = transitleastsquares(time, flux)\nresults = model.power()\n\n# Best period\nbest_period = results.period\nbest_t0 = results.T0\nbest_depth = results.depth\nsde = results.SDE  # Signal Detection Efficiency (SNR-like)\n\nprint(f\"Period: {best_period:.5f} days\")\nprint(f\"SDE: {sde:.1f}\")  # SDE > 8-9 is significant\n```\n\n## Period Grid Considerations\n\n### Nyquist-like Sampling\n```python\n# Minimum resolvable period based on cadence\ncadence = np.median(np.diff(time))\nmin_period = 2 * cadence\n\n# Oversample period grid\noversampling = 5\nn_periods = int(oversampling * baseline / min_period)\nperiods = np.linspace(min_period, baseline/2, n_periods)\n```\n\n### Logarithmic Grid (for wide ranges)\n```python\nperiods = np.logspace(np.log10(0.5), np.log10(20), 5000)\n```\n\n## Handling Harmonics\nBLS can find harmonics (P/2, P/3) or aliases (2P, 3P).\n\n```python\ndef check_harmonics(period, all_peaks, tol=0.01):\n    \"\"\"Check if period is a harmonic of a stronger signal.\"\"\"\n    harmonics = [period*2, period*3, period/2, period/3]\n    for h in harmonics:\n        for peak in all_peaks:\n            if abs(h - Processing these lightcurves involves filtering, detrending, and signal extraction.\n\n## Data Format\nTypical TESS lightcurve columns:\n- **Time**: Usually in MJD (Modified Julian Date) or BJD-TDB\n- **Flux**: Normalized or relative flux values\n- **Quality flags**: 0 = good data, non-zero = potentially problematic\n- **Flux uncertainty**: Error on each measurement\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install numpy pandas matplotlib scipy\n```\n\n## Loading and Filtering Data\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Load data\ndata = np.loadtxt('tess_lc.txt', skiprows=4)  # Skip header rows\ntime = data[:, 0]\nflux = data[:, 1]\nquality = data[:, 2]\nflux_err = data[:, 3]\n\n# Filter by quality flag (keep only good data)\ngood_mask = quality == 0\ntime = time[good_mask]\nflux = flux[good_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[good_mask]\n\n# Remove NaN values\nvalid_mask = ~np.isnan(flux) & ~np.isnan(time)\ntime = time[valid_mask]\nflux = flux[valid_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[valid_mask]\n```\n\n## Outlier Removal\n\n### Sigma Clipping\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef sigma_clip(data, sigma=3, maxiters=5):\n    \"\"\"Remove outliers using iterative sigma clipping.\"\"\"\n    mask = np.ones(len(data), dtype=bool)\n    for _ in range(maxiters):\n        mean = np.nanmean(data[mask])\n        std = np.nanstd(data[mask])\n        mask = np.abs(data - mean) < sigma * std\n    return mask\n\n# Apply sigma clipping to flux\nclip_mask = sigma_clip(flux, sigma=5)\ntime = time[clip_mask]\nflux = flux[clip_mask]\nflux_err = flux_err[clip_mask]\n```\n\n### MAD-based Clipping (more robust)\n```python\ndef mad_clip(data, threshold=5):\n    \"\"\"Remove outliers using Median Absolute Deviation.\"\"\"\n    median = np.median(data)\n    mad = np.median(np.abs(data - median))\n    mad_sigma = 1.4826 * mad  # Convert MAD to sigma equivalent\n    mask = np.abs(data - median) < threshold * mad_sigma\n    return mask\n```\n\n## Normalization\n```python\n# Normalize flux to median\nmedian_flux = np.median(flux)\nflux_norm = flux / median_flux\nflux_err_norm = flux_err / median_flux\n```\n\n## Time Handling\n```python\n# Convert to relative time (from start of observations)\ntime_rel = time - time.min()\n\n# Calculate time baseline\nbaseline = time.max() - time.min()  # Total observation span in days\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Detrending removes these long-term variations while preserving short-duration transit events.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install numpy scipy wotan\n# Optional: pip install celerite2 george\n```\n\n## Methods\n\n### 1. Savitzky-Golay Filter\nGood for smooth, slowly-varying trends.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.signal import savgol_filter\n\ndef detrend_savgol(flux, window_length=101, polyorder=3):\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply Savitzky-Golay filter for detrending.\n    window_length must be odd and > polyorder\n    \"\"\"\n    # Ensure window is odd\n    if window_length % 2 == 0:\n        window_length += 1\n\n    trend = savgol_filter(flux, window_length, polyorder)\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 2. Moving Median Filter\nSimple and robust to outliers.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.ndimage import median_filter\n\ndef detrend_median(flux, size=101):\n    \"\"\"Detrend using moving median.\"\"\"\n    trend = median_filter(flux, size=size)\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 3. Wotan Library (Recommended for Exoplanets)\nSpecialized library for transit photometry detrending.\n\n```python\nfrom wotan import flatten\n\n# Biweight filter (robust to transits)\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='biweight',\n                           window_length=0.5,  # days\n                           return_trend=True)\n\n# Alternative: Gaussian Process\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='gp',\n                           kernel='matern',\n                           kernel_size=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 4. Spline Fitting\nFlexible polynomial fitting.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.interpolate import UnivariateSpline\n\ndef detrend_spline(time, flux, s=None):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detrend using smoothing spline.\n    s: smoothing factor (None = automatic)\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by time\n    sort_idx = np.argsort(time)\n    time_sorted = time[sort_idx]\n    flux_sorted = flux[sort_idx]\n\n    # Fit spline\n    spline = UnivariateSpline(time_sorted, flux_sorted, s=s)\n    trend = spline(time)\n\n    detrended = flux / trend\n    return detrended, trend\n```\n\n### 5. Iterative Detrending (Masking Transits)\nFor better results when transits are visible.\n\n```python\ndef iterative_detrend(time, flux, window=0.5, n_iter=3, sigma=3):\n    \"\"\"\n    Iteratively detrend while masking transit-like dips.\n    \"\"\"\n    mask = np.ones(len(flux), dtype=bool)\n\n    for _ in range(n_iter):\n        # Fit trend only to non-transit points\n        from wotan import flatten\n        detrended, trend = flatten(time[mask], flux[mask],\n                                   method='biweight',\n                                   window_length=window,\n                                   return_trend=True)\n\n        # Interpolate trend to all points\n        from scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n        trend_func = interp1d(time[mask], trend,\n                             fill_value='extrapolate')\n        full_trend = trend_func(time)\n\n        # Update mask (exclude deep dips)\n        residual = flux / full_trend\n        median_res = np.median(residual)\n        std_res = np.std(residual)\n        mask = residual > median_res - sigma * std_res\n\n    return flux / full_trend, full_trend\n```\n\n## Choosing Window Size\n- **Too small**: Will remove transit signals\n- **Too large**: Won't capture stellar variability\n- **Rule of thumb**: Window should be ~3-5x transit duration\n- For unknown transits: Start with 0.5-1.0 days\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use methods robust to outliers (biweight, median)\n2. Check that transits aren't being removed\n3. Compare multiple detrending methods\n4. Window size should match stellar variability timescale\n5. For periodic stellar signals, consider fitting and removing sinusoids first\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

It's specifically designed for box-shaped (transit-like) signals that are brief compared to the orbital period.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install astropy scipy numpy\n# Optional: pip install transitleastsquares\n```\n\n## Using Astropy's BLS\n\n### Basic Usage\n```python\nfrom astropy.timeseries import BoxLeastSquares\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Create BLS model\nmodel = BoxLeastSquares(time, flux, dy=flux_err)\n\n# Define period grid\n# Rule: minimum period > 2x longest gap in data\n# Maximum period < half the baseline\nbaseline = time.max() - time.min()\nperiods = np.linspace(0.5, baseline/2, 10000)\n\n# Or use automatic period grid\nperiods = model.autoperiod(duration=np.linspace(0.01, 0.1, 10),\n                           minimum_period=0.5,\n                           maximum_period=baseline/2)\n\n# Run BLS\nresults = model.power(periods, duration=np.linspace(0.01, 0.1, 10))\n\n# Best period\nbest_period = results.period[np.argmax(results.power)]\nbest_t0 = results.transit_time[np.argmax(results.power)]\nbest_duration = results.duration[np.argmax(results.power)]\nbest_depth = results.depth[np.argmax(results.power)]\n```\n\n### Interpreting Results\n```python\n# Get statistics\nstats = model.compute_stats(best_period, best_duration, best_t0)\nprint(f\"Period: {best_period:.5f} days\")\nprint(f\"Transit depth: {best_depth:.4f}\")\nprint(f\"Duration: {best_duration*24:.2f} hours\")\nprint(f\"SNR: {stats['depth_snr']:.1f}\")\n```\n\n### Plotting BLS Periodogram\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\nfig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 4))\nax.plot(results.period, results.power, 'k-', lw=0.5)\nax.axvline(best_period, color='red', alpha=0.5, label=f'Best: {best_period:.4f} d')\nax.set_xlabel('Period (days)')\nax.set_ylabel('BLS Power')\nax.legend()\nplt.savefig('bls_periodogram.png', dpi=150)\n```\n\n### Phase Folding\n```python\ndef phase_fold(time, period, t0=0):\n    \"\"\"Phase fold time series.\"\"\"\n    phase = ((time - t0) % period) / period\n    phase[phase > 0.5] -= 1  # Center transit at phase 0\n    return phase\n\n# Fold on best period\nphase = phase_fold(time, best_period, best_t0)\n\n# Plot folded lightcurve\nplt.figure(figsize=(10, 4))\nplt.scatter(phase, flux, s=1, alpha=0.5)\nplt.xlabel('Phase')\nplt.ylabel('Normalized Flux')\nplt.title(f'Phase-folded at P = {best_period:.5f} days')\nplt.savefig('phase_folded.png', dpi=150)\n```\n\n## Using Transit Least Squares (TLS)\nMore sensitive than BLS, uses realistic transit shape.\n\n```python\nfrom transitleastsquares import transitleastsquares\n\n# Run TLS\nmodel = transitleastsquares(time, flux)\nresults = model.power()\n\n# Best period\nbest_period = results.period\nbest_t0 = results.T0\nbest_depth = results.depth\nsde = results.SDE  # Signal Detection Efficiency (SNR-like)\n\nprint(f\"Period: {best_period:.5f} days\")\nprint(f\"SDE: {sde:.1f}\")  # SDE > 8-9 is significant\n```\n\n## Period Grid Considerations\n\n### Nyquist-like Sampling\n```python\n# Minimum resolvable period based on cadence\ncadence = np.median(np.diff(time))\nmin_period = 2 * cadence\n\n# Oversample period grid\noversampling = 5\nn_periods = int(oversampling * baseline / min_period)\nperiods = np.linspace(min_period, baseline/2, n_periods)\n```\n\n### Logarithmic Grid (for wide ranges)\n```python\nperiods = np.logspace(np.log10(0.5), np.log10(20), 5000)\n```\n\n## Handling Harmonics\nBLS can find harmonics (P/2, P/3) or aliases (2P, 3P).\n\n```python\ndef check_harmonics(period, all_peaks, tol=0.01):\n    \"\"\"Check if period is a harmonic of a stronger signal.\"\"\"\n    harmonics = [period*2, period*3, period/2, period/3]\n    for h in harmonics:\n        for peak in all_peaks:\n            if abs(h - peak) / peak < tol:\n                return True, peak\n    return False, None\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Remove stellar variability BEFORE BLS\n2. Use fine period grid (oversampling ~5)\n3. Test multiple transit durations\n4. Check for harmonics and aliases\n5. Phase-fold to visually confirm transit\n6. SDE/SNR > 8-9 is typically significant\n7. Check for secondary eclipses at phase 0.5\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

window_length: Must be odd, larger = smoother\n# - polyorder: Polynomial order, must be < window_length\nsmoothed = savgol_filter(data, window_length=51, polyorder=3)\n```\n\n### Median Filter\nRobust to outliers and spikes.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.ndimage import median_filter\nsmoothed = median_filter(data, size=51)\n```\n\n### Gaussian Filter\nSmooth with Gaussian kernel.\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter1d\nsmoothed = gaussian_filter1d(data, sigma=10)\n```\n\n## Periodogram and Period Finding\n\n### Lomb-Scargle Periodogram\nFor unevenly sampled data (common in astronomy).\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.signal import lombscargle\n\n# Define frequency grid\nfreq = np.linspace(0.01, 10, 10000)  # cycles per day\nangular_freq = 2 * np.pi * freq\n\n# Compute periodogram\npower = lombscargle(time, flux, angular_freq)\n\n# Find peak period\nbest_freq = freq[np.argmax(power)]\nbest_period = 1 / best_freq\n```\n\n### Astropy Lomb-Scargle (more features)\n```python\nfrom astropy.timeseries import LombScargle\n\n# Create model\nls = LombScargle(time, flux, flux_err)\n\n# Compute power\nfreq, power = ls.autopower(minimum_frequency=0.01,\n                           maximum_frequency=10,\n                           samples_per_peak=10)\n\n# Best period\nbest_period = 1 / freq[np.argmax(power)]\n\n# False alarm probability\nfap = ls.false_alarm_probability(power.max())\n```\n\n## Detrending\n\n### Remove Polynomial Trend\n```python\nfrom numpy.polynomial import polynomial as P\n\n# Fit polynomial\ncoefs = P.polyfit(time, flux, deg=3)\ntrend = P.polyval(time, coefs)\ndetrended = flux - trend\n# Or for multiplicative: detrended = flux / trend\n```\n\n### Remove Sinusoidal Trend\n```python\nfrom scipy.optimize import curve_fit\n\ndef sinusoid(t, amp, period, phase, offset):\n    return amp * np.sin(2*np.pi*t/period + phase) + offset\n\n# Fit stellar rotation signal\npopt, pcov = curve_fit(sinusoid, time, flux,\n                       p0=[0.01, 5.0, 0, 1.0])  # initial guess\nstellar_signal = sinusoid(time, *popt)\ndetrended = flux / stellar_signal\n```\n\n## Interpolation for Gaps\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.interpolate import interp1d\n\n# Create interpolator\nf = interp1d(time, flux, kind='linear', fill_value='extrapolate')\n\n# Interpolate to new time grid\nnew_time = np.linspace(time.min(), time.max(), 10000)\ninterp_flux = f(new_time)\n```\n\n## Binning Data\n\n```python\nfrom scipy.stats import binned_statistic\n\n# Bin by time\nbin_means, bin_edges, _ = binned_statistic(time, flux,\n                                            statistic='mean',\n                                            bins=100)\nbin_centers = 0.5 * (bin_edges[:-1] + bin_edges[1:])\n```\n\n## Peak Finding\n```python\nfrom scipy.signal import find_peaks\n\n# Find peaks in periodogram\npeaks, properties = find_peaks(power, height=0.5*power.max(),\n                                distance=10)\npeak_periods = 1 / freq[peaks]\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

It implements multiple detrending methods optimized for exoplanet detection.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install wotan\n```\n\n## Basic Usage\n\n```python\nfrom wotan import flatten\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Load your data\ntime = ...  # Time array (days)\nflux = ...  # Flux array (normalized)\n\n# Simple detrending\ndetrended_flux, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                                 method='biweight',\n                                 window_length=0.5,\n                                 return_trend=True)\n```\n\n## Available Methods\n\n### 1. Biweight (Recommended for Transits)\nRobust to outliers including transit dips.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='biweight',\n                           window_length=0.5,  # days\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 2. Median Filter\nSimple and robust.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='median',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 3. Mean Filter\nStandard moving average.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='mean',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 4. Gaussian Process (GP)\nFlexible, captures complex variability.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='gp',\n                           kernel='matern',\n                           kernel_size=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 5. Spline\nPolynomial spline fitting.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='spline',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 6. Huber Spline\nRobust spline resistant to outliers.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='hspline',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### 7. Lowess\nLocally weighted scatterplot smoothing.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='lowess',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n## Important Parameters\n\n### window_length\n- Controls smoothing scale (in same units as time, usually days)\n- **Too small**: Removes transit signals\n- **Too large**: Doesn't remove stellar variability\n- **Typical range**: 0.3 - 1.0 days for short-period planets\n\n### break_tolerance\nHandle gaps in time series.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='biweight',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           break_tolerance=0.5,  # days\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n### edge_cutoff\nRemove edge effects.\n\n```python\ndetrended, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                           method='biweight',\n                           window_length=0.5,\n                           edge_cutoff=0.2,  # days\n                           return_trend=True)\n```\n\n## Handling Multiple Sectors\n\n```python\n# Detrend each sector separately\nsectors = np.unique(sector_array)\ndetrended_all = np.zeros_like(flux)\n\nfor sec in sectors:\n    mask = sector_array == sec\n    detrended_all[mask], _ = flatten(time[mask], flux[mask],\n                                      method='biweight',\n                                      window_length=0.5,\n                                      return_trend=True)\n```\n\n## Comparison Example\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nfrom wotan import flatten\n\nmethods = ['biweight', 'median', 'gp', 'hspline']\nfig, axes = plt.subplots(len(methods)+1, 1, figsize=(12, 10))\n\n# Original\naxes[0].scatter(time, flux, s=1)\naxes[0].set_title('Original')\n\n# Each method\nfor i, method in enumerate(methods):\n    try:\n        det, trend = flatten(time, flux,\n                             method=method,\n                             window_length=0.5,\n                             return_trend=True)\n        axes[i+1].scatter(time, det, s=1)\n        axes[i+1].set_title(method)\n    except:\n        axes[i+1].set_title(f'{method} (failed)')\n\nplt.tight_layout()\nplt.savefig('method_comparison.png')\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Biweight is preferred** for exoplanet work (robust to transits)\n2. Start with window_length = 0.5 days for typical hot Jupiters\n3. Use break_tolerance for data with gaps\n4. Always visually inspect the trend to ensure it's not fitting transits\n5. Compare multiple methods when unsure\n6. For strong stellar activity, may need larger window_length\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
